## 9. Stage 2 rulings — book first, then the field (2026-09-15, ACCEPTED by Michael same day)

> Five questions the standing-plan path needs answered before any fix touches them. Each was researched in this order: the book (`docs/SOURCE-viada-hybrid-athlete.md`, page cited), then the vendor's own help page (URL cited), then the code as it stands, then prior D-entries and ledger rows. **Status: ACCEPTED 2026-09-15 (all five, the three forks as recommended, the three easy-pace lines as printed).** Each gets its D-entry and ledger row in Stage 5, and the fix in Stage 3. The race-projection question in the workorder is parked with the race path (§3a).

| # | Question | Ruling in one line | Source | Copy changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | A lift's three numbers | They are meant to differ. THE number is the **1RM on file** (locked → trusted learned → typed). The State tile's e1RM is a receipt of the latest set; the working weight is ×0.96 of the 1RM and is never a headline. The one fracture: the learned aggregate and the State tile read different trusted rows; make them read the same. | p214/p215; Strong/Hevy; Juggernaut | none |
| Q2 | Easy pace | Easy pace is **one range off the accepted threshold, ×1.14 to ×1.29** (Friel run Zone 2), on every screen. The ×1.19 point goes. "From runs" is not a proposal; the measured value stays as the State receipt and the checkpoint's evidence. | Friel via TrainingPeaks; p235 (book: by feel) | yes, below |
| Q3 | Average heart rate | THE average is the **provider's number** (`workouts.avg_heart_rate`), what the watch and Garmin/Strava show. The sample mean is the fallback when the provider sent none. One resolver, every screen. | Garmin, Strava; book silent | none |
| Q4 | Ride efficiency factor | **Judged power ÷ whole-ride average heart rate, 2 dp**, computed once on the server. The pedalling-only denominator goes. | TrainingPeaks EF; intervals.icu; book silent | none |
| Q5 | Tested max | One number: the test set through the p215 formula, **rounded once to the nearest 5 lb**. The floor goes. One set-pick rule (the save's). Updated/kept is the athlete's tap, read back off the file. "Last test" compares to the previous **test** session only. | p215; ledger row 27 | none (the number can read 5 lb higher) |

**Open forks (one line each, recommendation first):**
- Q1: learned 1RM on file = max of the last 84 days (keep; matches Hevy "Best 1RM", a light day cannot drop it) vs latest trusted e1RM.
- Q2: keep a pace range on the plan's easy step beside the heart-rate range (keep; Garmin and TrainingPeaks print both) vs heart rate only (the book prints only the talk test).
- Q5: read updated/kept off the file (keep; no new column) vs store the tap on the workout row.

**Athlete-facing words that change (Q2 only) — for Michael, word for word:**
- Adjust / Baselines row value: `Easy pace 9:07–10:19/mi · from threshold` (was `12:40/mi · from runs` or `· from threshold`; numbers are an 8:00 threshold example).
- Adjust / Baselines row note: `Your zone 2 pace, worked out from your threshold pace. Easy days run by heart rate; this is the pace that usually lands there. Heat and hills slow it at the same heart rate.`
- Plan easy step reference: `129–135 bpm · about 9:07–10:19/mi` (was `… · about 9:31/mi`; exact step wording to be read off the materializer before the build).
- State row unchanged.

**Supersedes when accepted:** Q1 none (D-459 stands). Q2 amends D-462 (×1.19 → the 1.14–1.29 range) and rewrites ledger row 88. Q3 supersedes the computed-first order of D-182/D-185 for average heart rate only. Q4 none (ledger row 15 gains the denominator and 2 dp). Q5 none (D-339, D-342 stand; ledger row 27 gains `save-baseline-test` as a reader).

**OURS after these rulings:** Q1 the 84-day window, 3/6-row confidence cut, 42-day freshness, max-of-window. Q3 the fallback order. Q5 test-to-test comparison, outcome read off the file, line omitted with no prior test. Q2 the choice to print a pace range under the heart-rate prescription. Q4 nothing.

### 9.1 Full writeups

#### Q1 — A lift shows three numbers (Adjust/Baselines/My Record capacity · State e1RM tile · the plan's working number / training max). Are they meant to differ, what is each called, where is each shown?

**What the book says** (I read `docs/SOURCE-viada-hybrid-athlete.md` Part H :1498-1540, the p214/p215 verbatim block :1966-2015, p122 :432-450, p247 E1c/E1d :1260-1300, p163/p127 pointers :1408-1416, :1810-1818):
- **p214** — he names TWO numbers for one lift: the *"true 1-rep max"* and the *"working 1-rep max, which I define as (roughly) 96 percent of your true 1-rep max"*; the true max comes from a tested 5–6 rep max.
- **p215** — the test set goes through Epley and Brzycki, averaged, giving the *"predicted true 1RM"*; ×0.96 is the *"training 1-rep max"*. Worked example: 225 true, 215 training. So the book itself prints two different numbers for one lift on one page, by design.
- **p122** — he calls the programming number a *"training max"*, *"typical day potential"*, and shows Brzycki applied to an ordinary training set to estimate a max — the same operation as an e1RM off a logged set.
- **p247** — progression is *"slow gradual increases in the calculated 1RM … every 3 to 4 weeks (1 percent every 3 weeks)"*; the lower body carries a *"3 to 4 percent reduction in working 1RM"* that phases out. The number that moves is the calculated 1RM; the working number follows it.
- **p163 / p127** — a hybrid athlete's working max may sit *"double-digit percentage below their true max"*; cycling blocks lower it *"a few more percentage points"*. Both confirm the working number is a deliberately lower, separate quantity.
- The book gives no window, no sample count, no freshness rule for how a running estimate from logged sets becomes the number on file (grep `84\|42 d\|three logged\|sample` in SOURCE-viada: none on this topic).

**What the apps do:**
- **Strong** — one *"estimate of your best possible single rep lift"* per exercise, Brzycki, *"1RMs are always estimates"*, breaks down above 12 reps. I read https://help.strongapp.io/article/133-1rm.
- **Hevy** — per exercise: *"the heaviest weight used, your true or projected 1RM"*, *"best set and session volume"*, set records by rep count; "Best 1RM" is the highest ever. I read https://www.hevyapp.com/features/exercise-performance/ (the help-centre 1RM and PR articles returned 403; their search snippets say the same).
- **Juggernaut** — programs run off a *training max* set at 90% of the 1RM, recalculated from the all-out set; the JTS article's rule is *"lessen the 1rm when making a program"*. I read https://www.jtsstrength.com/redefining-the-one-rep-max/ (the 90% figure is from search snippets of the program guides, not a JTS help page).
- RP Hypertrophy and TrainingPeaks strength: no vendor page read; I am inferring nothing from them.
- Pattern: log apps show an estimated 1RM plus a best/record; program apps show a training max that is a stated fraction below the 1RM. No app collapses the two into one number.

**What the code does today:**
- `supabase/functions/_shared/state-trend/capacity-resolver.ts:128-179` — the number on file: locked → trusted learned (≥3 rows, medium/high, last logged ≤42 d; `:110-119`) → typed. The learned value is `compute-facts` max of trusted `exercise_log.estimated_1rm` rows over 84 d, reps ≤10 (`compute-facts/index.ts:912-955`, `strength-facts-lib.ts:319-347`). Shown on Adjust (`src/components/context/StateAdjustLens.tsx:120-123`, "Squat 225 lb · auto"), Baselines (`src/components/TrainingBaselines.tsx:1325-1339`, section "Lifts · 1RM"), My Record (`supabase/functions/athletic-record/record.ts:111` → `_shared/baseline-suggestions.ts:62-80`).
- `supabase/functions/_shared/state-trend/assemble.ts:410-502, 1376-1405` — the tile: `latestE1rm` = the newest trusted e1RM (D-417 rep ceiling 8, deadlift 5, heavy-intent sets only), `allTimeBestE1rm` = best ever, PR if latest ≥ best and ≥3 readings. Shown on State under the header "estimated 1-rep max" with labels `e1RM` and `best` (`src/components/context/StatePerformanceSection.tsx:561, 628-629`) plus the 12-week line and "+N since block".
- `supabase/functions/_shared/standing-plan/working-number.ts:35, 159-197, 213-230` — the Standing plan's working number = predicted 1RM × 0.96 (from the test set, or from the number on file via `workingNumberFromFile`). Shown once, on the test-done sheet (`StrengthLogger.tsx:6654` → `src/lib/standing-plan-copy.ts:63`): "Bench Press — 190 lb × 6 sets the working weight at 215 lb (about 96% of the tested max)." After that it appears only as the prescribed set weights.
- `supabase/functions/shared/strength-system/strength-primary-plan.ts:2958-2963` + `rematerialize-strength-block/index.ts:169` — Get Stronger's training max = 85% of the 1RM on file (`wendler-531.ts:205`), stored in `plans.config.training_max`, stepped per cycle. Shown as "Back Squat — climbing, training max 205 lb" (`src/lib/strength-calibration-copy.ts:156`) on State, Performance and the logger.
- The two readers of "what do the logged sets say the 1RM is" use different gates: `compute-facts` reps ≤10 over 84 d, any set intent; `assemble.ts` D-417 ceiling (8 / 5 deadlift), heavy-intent sets only, latest not max.

**Prior rulings:** D-231 (archive) typed-wins — reversed by D-459 (locked > trusted learned > typed; `DECISIONS-LOG-3.md:1480`). D-303 (per-lift e1RM read referenced to the athlete's own best, Strong/Hevy shape). D-420 pillar 1 (the record is all-history). D-339 (one 1RM formula). D-417 (rep ceiling). D-229 (pull-ups are reps, outside this rule). D-213 is engine-fork policy, not about lift numbers. Ledger: STATE-SOURCES row 27 (e1RM formula, Viada p215), row 83 (×0.96, Viada p215), row 126 (3 sessions / 42 d / 5%, OURS). TRUTH-MAP row 232 and §7.5 rows 1/1b flag "one lift, three numbers" as TWO. `working-number.ts:1-17` header and SOURCE Part H2 already rule that the 96% working number and the 85% training max never convert into each other.

**Ruling proposed:** They are meant to differ, and the book prints two of them on one page: the calculated **1RM** and the **working number** at 96% of it (p214/p215); the field shows the same pair (Strong/Hevy e1RM + best; Juggernaut training max). THE number for a lift is the **1RM on file** — the capacity resolver's value (locked → trusted learned → typed, D-459) — because it is the one the plan is built from and the one the athlete can lock; Adjust, Baselines and My Record show it, labelled "1RM" with the existing "auto" / "your number" word. The State tile is not a rival 1RM: its "e1RM" is the latest logged set's estimate (a receipt) and "best" is the record (D-420); it stays under "estimated 1-rep max". The **working weight** is derived (×0.96, p215) and is shown only where the block is built (the test-done sheet) and as the prescribed set weights, never as a headline beside the 1RM; Get Stronger's **training max** (×0.85) keeps its own word and its own status line and does not convert. Nothing above is OURS except the way logged sets become the 1RM on file: the 84-day window, the 3/6-row confidence cut, the 42-day freshness, and "max of the window rather than the latest set" have no page and are marked OURS in the ledger. The one real fracture is two readers of the same quantity with different gates (`compute-facts` reps ≤10 / any intent / 84 d max; `assemble.ts` D-417 ceiling / heavy intent / latest) — the learned aggregate that feeds the number on file must take the same trusted rows the tile takes, so the "auto" 1RM can never be a number the State line would refuse.

**What changes:**
- `supabase/functions/compute-facts/index.ts:912-955` and `compute-facts/strength-facts-lib.ts:319-347` — the learned aggregate reads the same trusted rows as the tile (`estimateIsTrusted` rep ceiling, heavy-intent filter); window stays 84 d, max stays max.
- `docs/STATE-SOURCES.md` — one new row: "1RM on file = max of the trusted e1RMs in the last 84 days, at least 3 rows, last logged within 42 days — OURS"; row 27 and 83 unchanged.
- `docs/TRUTH-MAP.md` row 232 and §7.5 row 1b — flag reads "by design (p214/p215): 1RM on file · latest e1RM + best · working weight"; the TWO moves to the compute-facts vs assemble gate.
- No athlete-facing words change. The three labels already on screen are the names: "estimated 1-rep max" / "e1RM" / "best" (State), "1RM" + "auto" / "your number" (Adjust, Baselines, My Record), "working weight (about 96% of the tested max)" (test-done sheet), "training max" (Get Stronger status line).

**Open fork:** whether the learned 1RM on file should be the **max of the last 84 days** (today; a light day cannot drop it, matches Hevy "Best 1RM" and Garmin auto-detect holding) or the **latest trusted e1RM** (what the tile prints; tracks p247's 1%-per-3-weeks drift down as well as up). Recommend keep the max — p215 sets the number by a test at block start and p247 moves it slowly; a per-session latest would move the plan's weights on every session, which no program app does. Mark the max OURS.

#### Q2 — Easy pace: one range from accepted threshold on every screen? What does the "from runs" value become?

**What the book says:**
- p235 (VT1; I read `SOURCE-viada-hybrid-athlete.md:900-909`): an easy run is anything at or below VT1; the level is about duration, and "the precise percentage of threshold may vary with fatigue, hydration and environment"; use the talk test twice per run. The book does not print an easy pace or a pace percentage for easy days.
- p211 (talk test; I read `:1481-1489`): the book's own easy anchor is a VT1 ramp test recording pace, speed or watts AND heart rate at the last level you could recite through. Not built; the app's easy zone is Friel's % of threshold heart rate (D-462, noted on the page as OURS by choice).
- p107 (I read `:293-300`, `:407-412`): cardiac drift is a session-termination rule for easy/VT1 work (10%, 5% hybrid). It says nothing about how easy pace is set.
- p92 six-zone table (I read `:316-318`): Zone 2 tops out at VT1. Zones are defined by ventilatory thresholds, not by pace percentages.

**What the apps do:**
- Friel / TrainingPeaks: run pace Zone 2 = 114–129% of threshold pace; Zone 1 slower than 129% — I read http://www2.trainingbible.com/joesblog/labels/zones.html ("114% to 129% of FTPa"), https://alpfitness.com/knowledge-base/guide-to-using-training-zones/ (same numbers, attributed to the TrainingPeaks "Joe Friel for Running" calculator) and https://help.staminity.com/en/basics/intensity-zones.html (same table, "114-129% of LTPace"). The TrainingPeaks help page itself returned 403 from this container.
- Daniels / VDOT: easy is a range, 59–74% VO2max, and the calculator warns the athlete "may be up to 20 seconds per mile slower or faster than the specified pace on a given day" — I read https://vdoto2.com/calculator. Easy is never a single number to hold.
- Garmin: heart-rate zones can be set as %LTHR — I read https://www8.garmin.com/manuals/webhelp/GUID-E5C62F3F-DCE3-4197-8CA5-E419B2A55D12/EN-US/GUID-30C91919-943C-44E9-8048-901AC0881AEA.html; the Z2 ceiling at 89% LTHR comes from a search summary (fitnesscheck.co.uk), not a Garmin page I read.
- COROS: pace zones hang off threshold pace; Zone 2 "Aerobic Endurance" is described by conversation and steady effort, no percentages printed — I read https://coros.com/stories/coros-metrics/c/evolab-updated-pace-zones. COROS support pages returned 403.
- Common thread: the vendors prescribe easy by heart rate or by a pace RANGE off threshold; none asks the athlete to accept an easy pace.

**What the code does today:**
- `supabase/functions/_shared/state-trend/run.ts:148-163` — State row: median pace + heart rate of the classified easy group's last five runs; printed by `src/components/context/StatePerformanceSection.tsx:1124-1127,1165-1166` as "pace · N bpm".
- `supabase/functions/learn-fitness-profile/index.ts:858-892` — learner: median of the five most recent easy runs (talk-test RPE ≤ 3 or heart rate in Friel Z2), confidence high at five.
- `src/lib/resolve-current-run-pace.ts:164-203` — resolver: learned last-five median at high confidence first (source `learned`, not an estimate), else threshold × 1.19 (`src/lib/run-paces-from-threshold.ts:43`, ratio from `src/lib/run-threshold-from-easy.ts:36-60`, measured off the app's own VDOT pace table — a point, not Friel's range).
- `src/components/context/StateAdjustLens.tsx:443-449` — Adjust: "Easy pace 12:40/mi · from runs" with the note "Your pace at 129–135 bpm, your zone 2, over your last five easy runs. Heat and hills slow this pace at the same heart rate." or "Worked out from threshold until five easy runs are on file."
- `src/components/TrainingBaselines.tsx:1214` — Baselines run tab reads the same resolver.
- `supabase/functions/materialize-plan/index.ts:3955-3959,800-813` — the plan's easy step: heart-rate range (Friel Z2 off threshold HR, `_shared/endurance/hr-zones.ts:25-27` → `src/lib/friel-zones.ts`) plus one reference pace from the same resolver.
- Twelve more server readers of the resolver (`analyze-running-workout:344`, `compute-workout-analysis:875`, `compute-adaptation-metrics:226`, `create-goal-and-materialize-plan:360,2647`, `course-detail:277`, `generate-strength-plan:327`, `ArcSetupWizard.tsx:1380`) grade or plan against whichever number the resolver returns.

**Prior rulings:**
- D-462 (`docs/DECISIONS-LOG-3.md:1565`): easy is a heart-rate zone; easy pace = threshold × 1.19 as a reference band, never a prescription source, kept "as the industry's zone math".
- STATE-SOURCES row 88 (2026-09-05): last-five median first, threshold × 1.19 below five; "no accept step: easy prescribes nothing"; notes State's row and the learner can pick different runs and differ by seconds.
- STATE-SOURCES row 89: easy-step heart-rate range = Friel Z2, 85–89% of threshold HR, one table.
- `docs/AUDIT-plan-materialization-2026-09-02.md:171`: "the easy reference pace is one number (×1.19) inside Friel's zone-2 pace band (114–129%). Showing the band instead of a point = display change."
- The "plan approved on 2026-09-14" is not written down in docs: `grep -rn "114" docs/*.md | grep -i -E "129|zone|easy"` hits only the audit line above and the workorder line itself; `grep -rln "2026-09-14" docs/` returns nine files, none carrying an easy-range plan. The approval lives in chat only.

**Ruling proposed:**
THE number for easy is the accepted threshold pace, and easy pace is not a number but ONE RANGE off it: threshold × 1.14 to threshold × 1.29 (Friel run Zone 2; FIELD — Friel via TrainingPeaks, the same author whose heart-rate table already sets the easy heart-rate range). Every screen that prints an easy pace prints that range and nothing else: Adjust and Baselines show "9:07–10:19/mi · from threshold" (example at an 8:00 threshold), the plan's easy step keeps the heart-rate range first and shows the range as its reference pace, and every server reader that grades or plans off easy pace reads the range (its midpoint where one number is structurally required, marked as a range midpoint, not a measurement). The × 1.19 point constant goes; the book's silence on easy pace (p235: easy is by feel and duration, the percentage moves with heat, fatigue and hydration) and Daniels' ± 20 s/mi daily allowance are why a range and not a point. The measured "from runs" value does NOT become a proposal: there is nothing to accept, because accepting it would make a second pace anchor (the thing D-462 removed) and the book says the easy percentage is expected to move. It stays exactly where it already lives as a receipt — the State row "12:40/mi · 134 bpm" over the last five easy runs — and as the six-week checkpoint's evidence input (unchanged). Threshold is the only pace the athlete proposes-and-accepts; the easy range moves the moment a new threshold is accepted, and until a threshold exists the easy pace field is empty (heart-rate range only). This supersedes STATE-SOURCES row 88 (measured-first, × 1.19 fallback) and amends D-462's "× 1.19" to the 1.14–1.29 range; row 89 (heart-rate range) stands. OURS: nothing numeric; the choice to print a pace range under the heart-rate prescription rather than heart rate alone is ours (Garmin and TrainingPeaks both print a pace zone; the book prints only the talk test).

**What changes:**
- `src/lib/run-paces-from-threshold.ts` — returns an easy range (low, high) off threshold; the 1.19 constant and its comment block in `src/lib/run-threshold-from-easy.ts` come out.
- `src/lib/resolve-current-run-pace.ts` — easy resolver drops the learned tier; returns the range with `is_estimate: true`, source `derived-from-threshold`, or null.
- `src/components/context/StateAdjustLens.tsx:443-449`, `src/components/TrainingBaselines.tsx:1214` — print the range and the new note.
- `supabase/functions/materialize-plan/index.ts:800-813,3955` — easy step reference becomes the range.
- The twelve server readers listed above — read the range (midpoint where one number is needed); `learn-fitness-profile` easy block unchanged (checkpoint input).
- `docs/STATE-SOURCES.md` row 88 rewritten; D-462 back-annotated.
- Athlete-facing words that change (for Michael):
  - Adjust / Baselines row value: "Easy pace 9:07–10:19/mi · from threshold" (was "12:40/mi · from runs" / "· from threshold").
  - Adjust / Baselines row note: "Your zone 2 pace, worked out from your threshold pace. Easy days run by heart rate; this is the pace that usually lands there. Heat and hills slow it at the same heart rate." (was the two notes quoted above).
  - Plan easy step: "129–135 bpm · about 9:07–10:19/mi" (was "129–135 bpm · about 9:31/mi"; exact step wording to be read off the materializer before the build).
  - State row: unchanged ("12:40/mi · 134 bpm").

**Open fork (only if real):**
- Keep a pace reference on the plan's easy step, or heart rate only. Book: talk test and heart rate only (p211, p235). Apps: Garmin and TrainingPeaks print a pace zone beside the heart-rate zone. Recommend keeping the range: the athlete this app is for reads pace, and a range does not compete with the heart-rate prescription the way a point did.

#### Q3 — Which average heart rate is THE average heart rate: the provider's (`workouts.avg_heart_rate`) or the sample mean (`computed.overall.avg_hr`)?

**What the book says:**
- p107 (read the whole B3 passage, `docs/SOURCE-viada-hybrid-athlete.md:293-305`): cardiac drift is a session-termination rule — heart rate up 10% (5% for hybrid athletes) *at a given pace* within the session. It compares heart rate across the session, not a whole-session average; nothing there says how to average.
- p211 (H0.2, line 1481-1486): VT1 heart rate is "the heart rate you noted there" at the last talk-test level — a reading off the watch, not a computed mean.
- Six-week review (line 374): "lower heart rate at completion" — again a reading, not an average.
- No page defines how an activity's average heart rate is computed (grep `-i "heart rate|heart-rate| HR |bpm"` over the SOURCE file; nine hits, listed above and on lines 694, 2122, 2252, 2411, none about averaging). The book is silent on the question.

**What the apps do:**
- Garmin Connect · shows the device's own activity average; there is no "moving average HR" field, and users who want one are told to use the Ultra Run profile's rest laps. Paused time counts toward the average (user report, no Garmin staff reply). I read https://forums.garmin.com/apps-software/mobile-apps-web/f/garmin-connect-web/347618/how-to-show-average-moving-heart-rate and https://forums.garmin.com/outdoor-recreation/outdoor-recreation/f/fenix-6-series/305718/activity-heart-rate-continues-to-count-for-averages-and-intensity-minutes-even-when-activity-is-paused. Garmin's own FAQ page (support.garmin.com faq=FMKY5NYJJ71DbuPmFP4O7A) returned 403 to both WebFetch and curl; not read.
- Strava · shows "average and maximum heart rates" under the chart; the help page does not say it recomputes anything. I read https://support.strava.com/en-us/articles/15401762-heart-rate. On the intervals.icu thread below, users report Strava and Garmin Connect agreeing with each other.
- intervals.icu · recomputes from the stream: the developer says it "interpolates the HR data to 1 second per point ticks", and users report it 3–5 bpm lower than Strava and Garmin Connect on the same file. I read https://forum.intervals.icu/t/avg-hr-differs-intervals-vs-strava/3986 and https://forum.intervals.icu/t/activity-average-heart-rate-way-too-low-vs-garmin-connect-consistent-across-all-runs/130946.
- TrainingPeaks · the help page "How and Why to Recalculate Workout Metrics" (help.trainingpeaks.com article 204071794) returned a Cloudflare challenge to both WebFetch and curl; not read. The search snippet says averages are recalculated when a data channel is edited. I am inferring from that snippet only.

**What the code does today:**
- `supabase/functions/ingest-activity/index.ts:507,980` — writes `workouts.avg_heart_rate` from the provider's summary (Strava `average_heartrate`, Garmin `averageHeartRateInBeatsPerMinute`).
- `supabase/functions/ingest-activity/index.ts:676-680` — also writes `computed.overall.avg_hr` as a plain mean of every sample that has an HR value; no time weighting, no stop filtering.
- `supabase/functions/compute-workout-analysis/index.ts:1969-1974` (and 1887 for swims) — overwrites `computed.overall.avg_hr` with the same plain sample mean; samples come from `supabase/lib/analysis/sensor-data/extractor.ts normalizeSamples`, which keeps every sample (grep `paused|moving|stopped|skip` there: only the timer-field name mapping at 228-232). Garmin smart recording samples unevenly, so this mean is not time-weighted.
- `supabase/functions/_shared/fact-packet/queries.ts:177-181 getOverallAvgHr` — sample mean first, provider number as fallback. The run resolver (`_shared/run/run-scalars.ts:56`) reads this.
- `supabase/functions/_shared/session-detail/build.ts:818` — Performance pill: run resolver → `compOverall.avg_hr` → facts → row column. Sample mean wins.
- `supabase/functions/workout-detail/index.ts:1925` — Details tile: row column first, never the sample mean. Provider number wins.
- `src/components/TodaysEffort.tsx:576,1196` — Today: `overall.avg_hr` first, row column second. Sample mean wins.
- `supabase/functions/compute-facts/index.ts:1255-1263` — `facts.avg_hr` (feeds the efficiency factor) reads the row column first; line 1065 reads the sample mean first. Two orders inside one function.
- Net: the same session can print two averages, one on Details, another on Performance and Today.

**Prior rulings:**
- D-182 (swim; cited in `docs/AUDIT-continuity-2026-06-16.md:4,14`) — "computed.overall-primary" standing rule for the card/narrative/Details triangle.
- D-185 (`docs/AUDIT-continuity-2026-06-16.md:4`) — one run resolver; it inherits the computed.overall-first order for HR.
- No ruling on which of the two averages is the truth (grep `-i "avg_hr|avg hr|average heart|avg_heart_rate|average hr"` over `docs/DECISIONS-LOG.md`: hits at 386, 470, 541, 755, 2804, 4010 are about drift, easy-run gating and GAP, not the field). `docs/STATE-SOURCES.md` rows 15-16 (efficiency factor ÷ avg HR) and 149 name "avg HR" without saying which field.

**Ruling proposed:**
THE average heart rate is the provider's number, `workouts.avg_heart_rate` — the figure the athlete's watch and Garmin Connect / Strava already show. The sample mean in `computed.overall.avg_hr` becomes the fallback, used only when the provider sent no summary (manual .fit import, older rows). Grounds: the book is silent on averaging; Garmin and Strava show the device average and agree with each other; intervals.icu is the one vendor that recomputes and its forum shows it having to explain 3–5 bpm gaps; our own recompute is an unweighted mean over unevenly spaced samples, so it is the less defensible of the two. This supersedes the computed.overall-first order of D-182/D-185 for average heart rate only (pace, distance, GAP keep their order). After the change every screen — Details tile, Performance pill, Today, the efficiency factor, the easy-run gate, per-domain load — reads one resolver, `getOverallAvgHr`, with the order provider → sample mean, and prints the same bpm. p107 drift is unaffected; it reads the series, not the average. Marked OURS: the fallback order itself (provider first, sample mean second); no number is introduced.

**What changes:**
- `supabase/functions/_shared/fact-packet/queries.ts` — `getOverallAvgHr` order becomes row column → `overall.avg_hr` → `overall.avg_heart_rate`.
- `supabase/functions/workout-detail/index.ts` (line 1925) — Details tile reads `getOverallAvgHr` instead of the row column directly.
- `supabase/functions/_shared/session-detail/build.ts` (line 818) — collapse the four-rung chain to the resolver (swim keeps `completedSwimScalars.avgHr`, which is already the row column).
- `supabase/functions/compute-facts/index.ts` (lines 1065, 1255) — both read the resolver.
- `src/components/TodaysEffort.tsx` (lines 576, 1196) — read the row column first, or better, the server's `avg_hr` from the session detail so the client does no picking.
- `docs/STATE-SOURCES.md` — one new row: "Average heart rate = the provider's activity average; sample mean only when none" · FIELD (Garmin Connect, Strava) · OURS for the fallback order.
- Athlete-facing words: none change. Labels stay "Avg HR" / "bpm"; only the number can move by a few bpm on Performance and Today where the two fields disagreed.

**Open fork (only if real):**
- A. Provider first, sample mean as fallback (recommended). Matches the athlete's watch; no reprocessing of stored workouts; one resolver.
- B. Sample mean everywhere, made time-weighted with 1-second interpolation the way intervals.icu does it. One method for every source, but disagrees with the watch by a few bpm on every session and needs the analyzer re-run over all stored rows.

#### Q4 — Ride efficiency factor: which denominator, how many decimals

**What the book says:** Nothing on an efficiency factor. `grep -n -i "efficiency" docs/SOURCE-viada-hybrid-athlete.md` hits one line (p87–88, plyometrics and "movement efficiency/economy") — a different subject. `grep -n -i "per beat\|watts per\|power.*heart rate"` hits nothing on a power-to-heart-rate ratio. The nearest page is p107 (I read the passage at lines 293–300 and 407–410): cardiac drift is a session-termination rule — 10%, 5% for hybrid athletes — which is the drift number, not the efficiency number. The book is silent; commercial practice decides.

**What the apps do:**
- TrainingPeaks · EF = normalized power (bike) or normalized graded pace (run) divided by "average heart rate for the workout or selected workout segment"; meant for steady all-aerobic workouts, compared like-for-like over weeks · I read https://www.trainingpeaks.com/blog/efficiency-factor-and-decoupling/ (the help-centre article 204071724 returned 403 to me; the search snippet of it gives the same sentence).
- intervals.icu · "Efficiency" = normalised power ÷ average heart rate for the activity; the developer says he uses NP, not average power · I read https://forum.intervals.icu/t/power-hr-vs-efficiency/4968 and https://forum.intervals.icu/t/fitness-page-help-for-efficiency-diagram/58246.
- Cyklopedia (coach's worked example of the TrainingPeaks number) · NP ÷ average heart rate for the workout; every printed value is two decimals (1.38, 1.40, 1.44, 1.47, 1.51, 1.57 …) · I read https://cyklopedia.cc/cycling-tips/efficiency-factor/.
- TrainerRoad forum · NP over average power because EF is for steady rides; no vendor says the heart-rate average is pedalling-only · I read https://www.trainerroad.com/forum/t/why-does-efficiency-factor-use-normalized-power-and-not-average-power/27007.
- Garmin · no efficiency factor on Garmin Connect (I am inferring from the absence in every result of the two searches above; I read no Garmin page).
- Decimals: no vendor help page states a precision. Two decimals is what TrainingPeaks-derived examples print and what our own State screen already prints (`fmtEff` → `toFixed(2)`). I am inferring "2 dp" from those examples, not from a spec line.

**What the code does today:**
- `supabase/functions/_shared/cycling-v1/ride-physiology.ts:112-137` — keeps only seconds with HR > 0 AND power > 0 (pedalling), divides NP (or the pedalling-only average power when NP is missing) by the pedalling-only mean HR, rounds to 3 dp; needs ≥ 60 such seconds. Written by `compute-workout-analysis/index.ts:1571` into `computed.analysis.efficiency`.
- `supabase/functions/compute-facts/index.ts:1255-1264` — `normalized_power` (workout column, else analysis) ÷ `avg_hr` (workout column `avg_heart_rate`, i.e. the whole-ride device average), rounds to 2 dp; written to `ride_facts.efficiency_factor`.
- `supabase/functions/_shared/session-detail/build.ts:1622-1651` — the session Details row prints the analyser's number with `toFixed(2)`, only when the ride also has a decoupling figure (≥ 20 min).
- `supabase/functions/compute-snapshot/endurance-facts.ts:9-12` and `index.ts:299-300, 2188` — State reads `ride_facts.efficiency_factor` as stored, averages it per week, rounds 2 dp into `ride_efficiency_factor`; `_shared/state-trend/bike-fitness.ts:46` gates which rides feed the trend (≥ 10 min in the aerobic band, Garmin).
- `src/components/context/StrengthReadCards.tsx:318-320` — `fmtEff` prints rides at 2 dp, runs at 3 dp.
- Net: one ride carries two efficiency factors (Details vs State), and they differ whenever the athlete coasted with HR still recorded (the pedalling-only HR is higher, so Details' number is lower) or a ride had no NP.

**Prior rulings:** `docs/STATE-SOURCES.md` row 15: "Efficiency factor, ride = normalized power ÷ avg HR · compute-facts · FIELD — TrainingPeaks EF" (backs State's formula). Row 25: which rides feed the trend (Garmin ≥ 10 min aerobic) — a gate, not the formula; untouched here. D-372 item 1 (`docs/DECISIONS-LOG.md:4468-4480`): NP counts coasting as 0 W; the same entry records that "pedalling power and ride efficiency were already excluding non-pedalling on their own and did not" change — that is the fork this ruling closes. `WORKORDER-app-one-truth-2026-09-15.md:41` (§2): judged ride power = NP for ≥ 20 min, else average power, coasting = 0 W. No D-entry on the EF denominator or decimals: `grep -n -i "efficiency factor\|ride efficiency\|aerobic efficiency" docs/DECISIONS-LOG.md` returns lines 96, 768, 1172, 4479 — none rules the denominator.

**Ruling proposed:** THE efficiency factor is the judged ride power (WORKORDER §2: normalized power with coasting at 0 W for rides of 20 min or more, average power below that) divided by the ride's one average heart rate over the whole ride — the same average heart rate the Details header prints, whichever source Stage 3 item (3) settles for it — rounded to two decimals, computed once on the server and copied by every screen. That is TrainingPeaks' and intervals.icu's definition to the letter (FIELD); the two decimals are FIELD by example (every published EF value prints two) and match what State already shows. The pedalling-only heart-rate average goes: no vendor divides by it, and it made Details disagree with State on the same ride. After the change the Details row, the State bike row ("efficiency factor 1.xx"), the weekly snapshot and the coach's week-over-week read all print the identical number for a ride. Rides shorter than 20 minutes get an efficiency factor from average power (the §2 rule, already sourced to TrainingPeaks/TrainerRoad); the Details row stays hidden for them as today (it requires a decoupling figure, which needs 20 min), and the State trend keeps its ≥ 10 min aerobic gate (row 25). Nothing new is OURS; row 15 gains "whole-ride average heart rate, 2 dp, judged power per §2" and the ledger row for the analyser's pedalling-only HR (none exists) is not needed.

**What changes:**
- `supabase/functions/_shared/cycling-v1/ride-physiology.ts` — `computeRideEfficiency` divides by the whole-ride average heart rate (not the pedalling-only mean), takes the judged power from `_shared/ride-power.ts`, rounds 2 dp; `avg_pedaling_hr_bpm` is no longer the denominator (keep or drop the field — nothing on a screen reads it: `grep -rn avg_pedaling_hr src supabase/functions` hits only the writer). The decoupling half-split (first half vs second half of pedalling samples) is a separate number and is not touched.
- `supabase/functions/compute-workout-analysis/index.ts:1571` — passes the ride's average heart rate and the judged power in.
- `supabase/functions/compute-facts/index.ts:1262-1264` — copies `computed.analysis.efficiency.efficiency_factor` instead of recomputing (the endurance-facts rule "read as stored, never re-derived" then holds one step earlier); keeps the 2 dp.
- `docs/STATE-SOURCES.md` row 15 — add "whole-ride average heart rate · 2 dp · judged power per §2".
- `supabase/functions/compute-snapshot/index.test.ts`, `endurance-facts.test.ts` — fixtures unchanged (already 2 dp).
- Athlete-facing words: none change. The Details row text stays exactly: `Efficiency factor 1.23: power per heartbeat (normalized power ÷ average heart rate). Higher on the same kind of ride over time means fitter.` — it only ever shows for rides of 20 min or more, where the judged power is normalized power, so the words stay true. State's row label "efficiency factor" and its 2 dp value are unchanged.

**Open fork (only if real):** none. The one candidate — keep the pedalling-only heart-rate average because HR on a descent is "not earning power" — has no vendor behind it (none of the four pages read), and the drift read already handles the pedalling-vs-coasting question on its own.

#### Q5 — One test set produces two numbers and two verdicts

**What the book says:**
- p215 (I read docs/SOURCE-viada-hybrid-athlete.md Part H lines 1498–1535 and J6 lines 1966–1984; the page image is filed at book-sources/p215.png): the pretest is 75% of the predicted max for 6, +10% for 5, +5% more for max reps; the final set's load and reps go through Epley and Brzycki and the two are averaged — that is the "predicted true 1RM"; 96% of it is the training max. The worked example is 190 × 6 → 227.9 and 220.6 → average 224.25 → the page states it as "225 is your true max, and 215 training max" (215.52 → 215). So the book itself rounds the displayed max to the nearest 5, not down.
- p214 (same J6 passage): test a 5-rep max before the program; a 5- to 6-rep max gives the best reliability and precision.
- p120–122 (Part B7, lines 660–671; p122 at line 441): the training max is typical-day potential; progress without retesting; tests exist for troubleshooting. p122 gives Brzycki alone for illustrating workout targets, a different job from p215.
- The book says nothing about what to do when a retest comes in lower, nothing about which set counts if the athlete logs more than the protocol's three, and nothing about comparing one test to the previous test (grep "retest|re-test|last test|previous test" over the SOURCE file: hits at 671, 1204, 1549, 1781, 1801 only, none of them a rule).

**What the apps do:**
- Strong · one estimate per set, Brzycki, "1RMs are always estimates", breaks down above 12 reps; no rounding rule and no set-pick rule on the page · I read https://help.strongapp.io/article/133-1rm
- Hevy · an estimate for any set, e.g. 135 lb × 5 → 152 lb (that is Brzycki 151.9 rounded to the nearest whole pound, not to 5) · https://help.hevyapp.com/hc/en-us/articles/36954464726167 — the page returned 403 to fetch; the figures are from the search snippet of that page, not a full read.
- JuggernautAI · the training max is re-set from the AMRAP set's rep count at the end of each wave; the program frames the flagged all-out set as the measurement · I did not reach a vendor help page (search only: juggernautai.app/blog/juggernautai-25 was listed, not read). RP · nothing on rpstrength.com about e1RM or training-max update in search; not read.
- Summary: the field shows an estimate per set at fine rounding and treats the flagged all-out set as the measurement. No vendor page I read says how a lower retest is handled.

**What the code does today:**
- Formula: one already. `supabase/functions/save-baseline-test/index.ts:59` and `supabase/functions/compute-facts/strength-facts-lib.ts:51` both call `src/lib/estimate-1rm.ts estimate1RM` (Epley + Brzycki averaged, `:96–98`; a single returns as-is). `analyze-strength-workout` never computes; it reads `exercise_log.estimated_1rm` written by compute-facts.
- Rounding (a): `save-baseline-test/index.ts:58–62` floors to the 5 below (comment: "on purpose"); `strength-facts-lib.ts:51` → `estimate1RMRounded` (`estimate-1rm.ts:170–174`) rounds to the nearest 5; `analyze-strength-workout/index.ts:819–820` then `Math.round`s that. RIR fold: `strength-facts-lib.ts:198–206` folds reserve only when the exercise carries `rir_tracked: true` and the set's RIR was typed; the save path never folds. So 190 × 6 saves as 220 and shows as 225 on the test result.
- Set pick (b): `save-baseline-test/pick.ts:68–111` — completed working sets only; a row with a scored (AMRAP) set uses only that set; on a named "Baseline Test: Upper/Lower/Full" session an unscored set must carry a typed RIR of 2–3; among those the heaviest, flagged set on a tie, later on a tie (`_shared/standing-plan/working-number.ts:394–399`). `analyze-strength-workout/index.ts:770–775` — the flagged set, else the last completed working set, else the heaviest of any set including warm-ups. The two can name different sets, and the analyzer's e1RM comes from compute-facts's best set (`strength-facts-lib.ts` bestWeight/bestReps), a third pick.
- Updated/kept (c): `save-baseline-test/index.ts:172–191` — equal or higher writes; lower returns `needs_decision` and honours the athlete's Keep/Update tap (`src/components/StrengthLogger.tsx:1279–1283, 6679–6725`). `analyze-strength-workout/index.ts:806–813` recomputes updated/kept as "this session's e1RM ≥ stored", so a Kept lift can read "updated to" and a Keep tap is not visible on the result.
- "Last test" (d): `analyze-strength-workout/index.ts:722–738` takes the most recent prior `exercise_log` row with any e1RM for the lift — a normal training session, not a test — and `StrengthTestResult.tsx:82` labels it "last test".

**Prior rulings:**
- D-339 (docs/DECISIONS-LOG.md:2405) — one formula; amended 2026-09-01 so the one formula is `estimate1RM` (averaged). Stands; nothing here changes the formula.
- D-342 (DECISIONS-LOG.md:2596–2613) — the tested 1RM is computed and written by `save-baseline-test`, two-phase, nothing written until every Keep/Update is decided. Stands; this ruling extends it to the result screen.
- STATE-SOURCES.md row 27 — "e1RM = average of Epley and Brzycki, nearest 5 lb", FIELD, p215. Stands; the floor in save-baseline-test contradicts it today.
- The floor has no D-entry (grep "floor" over DECISIONS-LOG.md and STATE-SOURCES.md: no row for it); it lives only in the code comment at `save-baseline-test/index.ts:50–56`. D-223 (ratchet-up-only) and D-203/D-224 (the RIR 2–3 gate) are cited in code comments; `grep "^## D-223\|^## D-203\|^## D-224" docs/DECISIONS-LOG.md` finds no header for any of them.

**Ruling proposed:**
THE number is the tested max that `save-baseline-test` writes to `performance_numbers`: the test set's logged weight and reps through `estimate1RM` (p215, Epley and Brzycki averaged, no RIR fold — the page uses the reps performed), rounded once to the nearest 5 lb (p215's own example rounds 224.25 to 225; ledger row 27). The floor comes out: the prescription basis is already 96% of this number (p215), so rounding down first is a second haircut the page does not print. The test set is the one `pick.ts` names (heaviest completed working set; a scored set beats an unscored one; later on a tie; the RIR 2–3 gate on named Baseline Test sessions stands as it is) — the result screen must use the same pick and the same rounding, so the number the athlete sees on the result is the number that was saved, to the pound. Updated/kept is the athlete's decision and is read back off the file, not recomputed: after the save, a lift whose stored number equals this test's number shows "updated to N" (or "new baseline" when nothing was on file before); a stored number that differs shows "kept N". "Last test" compares to the previous test session for that lift (a session tagged `1rm_test` or named "Baseline Test", the same rule `pick.ts isTestSession` uses), never to a training session; with no earlier test the line is omitted. OURS, labelled: comparing test to previous test (the book only says tests are for troubleshooting, p120), reading the outcome off the file rather than storing the tap, and omitting the line when no prior test exists.

**What changes:**
- `supabase/functions/save-baseline-test/index.ts` — round to the nearest 5 (`estimate1RMRounded`), remove the "floor on purpose" comment.
- `supabase/functions/analyze-strength-workout/index.ts` — the test frame picks the set with `pickTestLifts`, computes e1RM with `estimate1RMRounded` from that set, sets outcome from `performance_numbers` after the save, and reads "last test" from the previous test session only (join on the session's tag/name, not any `exercise_log` row).
- `docs/STATE-SOURCES.md` — row 27 gains `save-baseline-test` as a second reader; one new row for the test-set pick and the test-to-test comparison, marked OURS where stated above.
- `src/components/StrengthTestResult.tsx` and `StrengthLogger.tsx` — no words change. Existing lines stay exactly: "updated to N", "kept N", "new baseline", "last test A → B lb", "stored P · tested N", "Keep P", "Update to N", "This test came in below what's on file.", "test set logged 0 reps — retest for a number". The only visible difference is that N can be 5 lb higher than today on a set whose estimate sat above the 5 below, and the result screen's N now matches the dialog's N.

**Open fork (only if real):**
- Outcome from the file vs storing the tap: reading the file needs no new column but depends on the save landing before the analysis runs (the logger already saves first; a recompute later still reads the file correctly). Storing the tap on the workout row is exact but adds a field and a second writer. Recommend the file.
